\big[{\mathbin{\cal M}}_{\boldsymbol{d}}^{\rm ss}(\mu)\big]_{\rm inv}
in Proposition
6.1
satisfy Theorem
5.8
(i) for all
\boldsymbol{d}\in{\mathbin{\mathbb{N}}}^{Q_{0}}\setminus\{0\}
with
|\boldsymbol{d}|=\sum_{v\in Q_{0}}\boldsymbol{d}(v)\leqslant\penalty k
. The first step
k=0
is vacuous. For the inductive step, suppose the inductive hypothesis holds for some
k\geqslant 0
, and let
\boldsymbol{d}\in{\mathbin{\mathbb{N}}}^{Q_{0}}\setminus\{0\}
with
|\boldsymbol{d}|=k+1
.

Consider the pair invariant set-up of Definition
5.12
. Suppose
\boldsymbol{d}_{1},\dots,\boldsymbol{d}_{n}
lie in
{\mathbin{\mathbb{N}}}^{Q_{0}}\setminus\{0\}
for
n\geqslant 2
with
\boldsymbol{d}_{1}+\cdots+\boldsymbol{d}_{n}=\boldsymbol{d}
and
\mu(\boldsymbol{d}_{i})=\mu(\boldsymbol{d})
. If
{\mathbin{\cal M}}_{\boldsymbol{d}_{i}}^{\rm ss}(\mu)\neq\varnothing
for all
i=1,\dots,n
then choosing a
{\mathbin{\mathbb{C}}}
-point
[E_{i}]
in
{\mathbin{\cal M}}_{\boldsymbol{d}_{i}}^{\rm ss}(\mu)
, we see that
E_{1}\oplus\cdots\oplus E_{n}
is strictly
\mu
-semistable in class
\boldsymbol{d}
, contradicting
{\mathbin{\cal M}}^{\rm st}_{\boldsymbol{d}}(\mu)={\mathbin{\cal M}}^{\rm ss}_{\boldsymbol{d}}(\mu)
. Hence
{\mathbin{\cal M}}_{\boldsymbol{d}_{i}}^{\rm ss}(\mu)=\varnothing
for some
i=1,\dots,n
, so
[{\mathbin{\cal M}}^{\rm ss}_{\boldsymbol{d}_{i}}(\mu)]_{\rm inv}=0
by the inductive hypothesis, since
|\boldsymbol{d}_{i}|\leqslant\penalty k
as
\boldsymbol{d}_{1}+\cdots+\boldsymbol{d}_{n}=\boldsymbol{d}
with
|\boldsymbol{d}|=k+1
,
n\geqslant 2
and
|\boldsymbol{d}_{j}|>0
. Hence in (
5.14
) all terms with
n\geqslant 2
are zero, so (
5.14
) reduces to

\tilde{\iota}_{*}\bigl(\big[{\mathbin{\smash{\kern 6.99997pt\widetilde{\kern-6.99997pt\mathcal{M}\kern-3.99994pt}\kern 3.99994pt}}}_{(\boldsymbol{d},1)}^{\rm ss}(\tilde{\mu}^{\boldsymbol{d}}_{+})\big]_{\rm fund}\bigr)=\bigl[i_{*}^{\rm pl}\bigl(\big[{\mathbin{\cal M}}_{\boldsymbol{d}}^{\rm ss}(\mu)\big]_{\rm inv}\bigr),1_{H_{0}({\mathbin{\smash{\kern 5.7361pt\widetilde{\kern-5.7361pt\mathcal{M}\kern-3.27773pt}\kern 3.27773pt}}}_{(0,1)}^{\rm pl})}\bigr],

(6.26)

that is, the ‘lower order terms’ in (
5.15
) vanish. Here we have used the fact that as
\tilde{\mu}^{\boldsymbol{d}}_{+}
,
(\boldsymbol{d},1)
are generic for
\mathop{\text{\rm mod-}{\mathbin{\mathbb{C}}}\tilde{Q}}
, Proposition
6.12
implies that

\big[{\mathbin{\smash{\kern 6.99997pt\widetilde{\kern-6.99997pt\mathcal{M}\kern-3.99994pt}\kern 3.99994pt}}}_{(\boldsymbol{d},1)}^{\rm ss}(\tilde{\mu}^{\boldsymbol{d}}_{+})\big]_{\rm inv}=\tilde{\iota}_{*}\bigl(\big[{\mathbin{\smash{\kern 6.99997pt\widetilde{\kern-6.99997pt\mathcal{M}\kern-3.99994pt}\kern 3.99994pt}}}_{(\boldsymbol{d},1)}^{\rm ss}(\tilde{\mu}^{\boldsymbol{d}}_{+})\big]_{\rm fund}\bigr),

which was used to rewrite the left-hand side of (
4.4
) to get (
5.14
).

There is a natural projection
\pi\colon{\mathbin{\smash{\kern 6.99997pt\widetilde{\kern-6.99997pt\mathcal{M}\kern-3.99994pt}\kern 3.99994pt}}}_{(\boldsymbol{d},1)}^{\rm ss}(\tilde{\mu}^{\boldsymbol{d}}_{+})\rightarrow{\mathbin{\cal M}}_{\boldsymbol{d}}^{\rm ss}(\mu)
which acts by

((V_{v})_{v\in\tilde{Q}_{0}},(\rho_{e})_{e\in\tilde{Q}_{1}})\longmapsto((V_{v})_{v\in Q_{0}},(\rho_{e})_{e\in Q_{1}})

on
{\mathbin{\mathbb{C}}}
-points, noting that
Q_{0}\subset\tilde{Q}_{0}
and
\tilde{Q}_{1}\subset Q_{1}
. That is,
\pi
forgets the vector space
V_{\infty}\cong{\mathbin{\mathbb{C}}}
and the edge maps
\rho_{(v,i)}\colon V_{\infty}\rightarrow V_{v}
for edges
\overset{\infty}{\bullet}\,{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\longrightarrow}\limits^{(v,i)}}}\,\overset{v}{\bullet}
in
\tilde{Q}
, where
v\in Q_{0}
and
i=1,\dots,n_{v}
. In terms of the exact sequence (
4.3
) in Definition
4.3
,
\pi
maps
[B]\mapsto[A]
on
{\mathbin{\mathbb{C}}}
-points. The fibre of
\pi
over a
{\mathbin{\mathbb{C}}}
-point
[(V_{v})_{v\in Q_{0}},(\rho_{e})_{e\in Q_{1}}]
is the projective space
{\mathbin{\mathbb{P}}}\big(\bigoplus_{v\in Q_{0}}V_{v}^{\oplus^{n_{v}}}\big)
parametrizing the forgotten edge maps
\rho_{(v,i)}
up to scale, where the rescalings come from changing the isomorphism
V_{\infty}\cong{\mathbin{\mathbb{C}}}
, and the condition of
\tilde{\mu}^{\boldsymbol{d}}_{+}
-semistability is that the image under
\pi
should be
\mu
-semistable, and the
\rho_{(v,i)}
should not all be zero. Thus we may identify
{\mathbin{\smash{\kern 6.99997pt\widetilde{\kern-6.99997pt\mathcal{M}\kern-3.99994pt}\kern 3.99994pt}}}_{(\boldsymbol{d},1)}^{\rm ss}\big(\tilde{\mu}^{\boldsymbol{d}}_{+}\big)
with the projective space bundle

{\mathbin{\smash{\kern 6.99997pt\widetilde{\kern-6.99997pt\mathcal{M}\kern-3.99994pt}\kern 3.99994pt}}}_{(\boldsymbol{d},1)}^{\rm ss}\big(\tilde{\mu}^{\boldsymbol{d}}_{+}\big)\cong{\mathbin{\mathbb{P}}}\bigg(\bigoplus_{v\in Q_{0}}{\cal V}_{v,\boldsymbol{d}}^{\oplus^{n_{v}}}\bigg)\rightarrow{\mathbin{\cal M}}_{\boldsymbol{d}}^{\rm ss}(\mu),

(6.27)

where the vector bundles
{\cal V}_{v,\boldsymbol{d}}\rightarrow{\mathbin{\cal M}}_{\boldsymbol{d}}
are as in Definition
5.2
. In fact
{\cal V}_{v,\boldsymbol{d}}
does not descend through
{\mathbin{\cal M}}_{\boldsymbol{d}}\rightarrow{\mathbin{\cal M}}_{\boldsymbol{d}}^{\rm pl}
to
{\mathbin{\cal M}}^{\rm pl}_{\boldsymbol{d}}\supseteq{\mathbin{\cal M}}_{\boldsymbol{d}}^{\rm ss}(\mu)
, but the projective bundle
{\mathbin{\mathbb{P}}}\bigl(\bigoplus_{v\in Q_{0}}{\cal V}_{v,\boldsymbol{d}}^{\oplus^{n_{v}}}\bigr)\rightarrow{\mathbin{\cal M}}_{\boldsymbol{d}}
does descend to
{\mathbin{\cal M}}_{\boldsymbol{d}}^{\rm pl}
, which is what we mean in (
6.27
).

Now the second author
[
58
]
gives an alternative, geometric definition of the Lie bracket
[\,,\,]
on
\check{H}_{*}\big({\mathbin{\cal M}}^{\rm pl}\big)
in Section
2.4
in terms of the ‘projective Euler class’
\mathop{\rm PE}(\Theta^{\bullet})
of the perfect complex
\Theta^{\bullet}\rightarrow{\mathbin{\cal M}}\times{\mathbin{\cal M}}
in Assumption
2.4
(g). For the Lie bracket in (
6.26
), the complex
\Theta^{\bullet}
for
\mathop{\text{\rm mod-}{\mathbin{\mathbb{C}}}\tilde{Q}}
is given in (
5.4
)–(
5.5
), and its restriction to
{\mathbin{\smash{\kern 6.99997pt\widetilde{\kern-6.99997pt\mathcal{M}\kern-3.99994pt}\kern 3.99994pt}}}_{(\boldsymbol{d},0)}\times{\mathbin{\smash{\kern 6.99997pt\widetilde{\kern-6.99997pt\mathcal{M}\kern-3.99994pt}\kern 3.99994pt}}}_{(0,1)}
reduces to

\Theta^{\bullet}|_{{\mathbin{\smash{\kern 5.7361pt\widetilde{\kern-5.7361pt\mathcal{M}\kern-3.27773pt}\kern 3.27773pt}}}_{(\boldsymbol{d},0)}\times{\mathbin{\smash{\kern 5.7361pt\widetilde{\kern-5.7361pt\mathcal{M}\kern-3.27773pt}\kern 3.27773pt}}}_{(0,1)}}\cong\bigg(\bigoplus_{v\in Q_{0}}{\cal V}_{v,\boldsymbol{d}}^{\oplus^{n_{v}}}\boxtimes{\cal V}_{\infty,1}^{*}\bigg)[-1],

(6.28)

where
{\cal V}_{\infty,1}^{*}\rightarrow{\mathbin{\smash{\kern 6.99997pt\widetilde{\kern-6.99997pt\mathcal{M}\kern-3.99994pt}\kern 3.99994pt}}}_{(0,1)}
is a line bundle. When
\Theta^{\bullet}=F[-1]
is a vector bundle
F
in degree
1
, the definition of
\mathop{\rm PE}(\Theta^{\bullet})
in
[
58
]
involves the projective bundle
{\mathbin{\mathbb{P}}}(F)
. Using this, one can show from (
6.27
)–(
6.28
) and
[
58
]
that

\tilde{\iota}_{*}\bigl(\big[{\mathbin{\smash{\kern 6.99997pt\widetilde{\kern-6.99997pt\mathcal{M}\kern-3.99994pt}\kern 3.99994pt}}}_{(\boldsymbol{d},1)}^{\rm ss}(\tilde{\mu}^{\boldsymbol{d}}_{+})\big]_{\rm fund}\bigr)=\bigl[i_{*}^{\rm pl}\bigl(\iota_{*}([{\mathbin{\cal M}}_{\boldsymbol{d}}^{\rm ss}(\mu)]_{\rm fund})\bigr),1_{H_{0}({\mathbin{\smash{\kern 5.7361pt\widetilde{\kern-5.7361pt\mathcal{M}\kern-3.27773pt}\kern 3.27773pt}}}_{(0,1)}^{\rm pl})}\bigr].

(6.29)

Comparing (
6.26
) and (
6.29
), and using the fact that
\big[{-},1_{H_{0}({\mathbin{\smash{\kern 5.7361pt\widetilde{\kern-5.7361pt\mathcal{M}\kern-3.27773pt}\kern 3.27773pt}}}_{(0,1)}^{\rm pl})}\big]
is injective if
n_{v}>0
for all
v\in Q_{0}
(see the argument after (
5.13
) above), and
i_{*}^{\rm pl}
is an isomorphism, we see that
\big[{\mathbin{\cal M}}_{\boldsymbol{d}}^{\rm ss}(\mu)\big]_{\rm inv}=\iota_{*}\big(\big[{\mathbin{\cal M}}_{\boldsymbol{d}}^{\rm ss}(\mu)\big]_{\rm fund}\big)
. This proves the inductive step, and the proposition follows by induction.
∎
